Interviewer[13]: Hi RamChetan, really glad we could connect today. I'm Ishitha, and I've been looking forward to our conversation about the AI/ML Engineer – Generative AI & Agentic Systems role. How are you doing? Hope the day's been good to you.
Candidate[16]: I'm doing great.
Interviewer[24]: Good to hear. Ready to get into it?
Candidate[28]: Yeah. Let's dive in.
Interviewer[38]: You mentioned the Pneumonia Detector project—what was your specific role in building that, and which part did you personally develop?
Candidate[56]: Basically, it was in a hackathon project where me and colleagues developed the project. main goal was to develop the front end of pneumonia detector. Used React framework to develop the front end part.
Interviewer[64]: When you worked on the React front end, what was the toughest challenge you faced to make sure it worked smoothly with the Flask backend?
Candidate[92]: The main thing the main issue was some widgets were not showing up properly and also it was a dynamic web page. So the based on the data, it was always changing the layout, and it was not aligning properly. Which I had issue with. Again, the color match and everything was bit new to me, and also React was also new at that moment to me.
Interviewer[104]: Given the tight timeline and learning curve with React, what features or polish did you decide to skip or simplify in the front end to meet your deadlines?
Candidate[126]: Basically, I managed to I only learned which are required at that moment. Like, what I will be involved in the team and also in the project. So only those are included and the rest
Interviewer[141]: Got it. Let's move to your VisionMouse -- the one where you hands-free cursor control system using real-time 3D eye gaze tracking and hand gesture recognition. What was your role there, and what did you actually build?
Candidate[166]: project. Basically, I developed the first version and second version of the VisionMOS. The second version of the Vision Mouse is three I guess, tracking. The first one was only eyelid control. I used OpenCV media pipe facemesh to control the cursor.
Interviewer[175]: Interesting progression there. How did you approach improving the tracking from eyelid only to the more advanced 3D tracking in VisionMouse?
Candidate[215]: Basically, the eyelet was only controlling the cursor when the person or the user is moving. It was not as we thought, and it was not really controlling the cursor using eyes. Later, we implemented the three tracking so that when the person moves his eyes or the pupil, then the person moves. So that's why we advanced it to three tracking using gaze prediction and EAR aspect ratio. Along with that, we implemented a 16 calibrated
Interviewer[237]: Makes sense. How did you handle the data processing and calibration to ensure accurate eye gaze tracking in VisionMouse?
Candidate[292]: tool. Basically, the data processing and collection is a first step in the project. The user opens up application, he has to do this. Like, 16 points are spread all over the world. All over the screen. And the 16 the user have to look at each point for certain milliseconds or seconds. Based on it based on the data we collected, we classify we decide the distance between user and the also how big is the screen. And based on it, later when the user looks at the screen, we have the data which we collected at the beginning. That's how we detect which point it is and control the cursor.
Interviewer[301]: Sounds like a solid calibration method. How did you handle gesture recognition alongside the eye gaze tracking?
Candidate[334]: Gesture recognition is basically hand it is used different library, which is a media pipe and slash, which is which recognizes six joints in our fingers and all over the hand. It was not really hard because the trained data sets and other training models were much more active and it was not a big hassle for us to integrate it with alongside with
Interviewer[348]: Okay. How did you integrate the eye gaze tracking and hand gesture recognition to work together smoothly in VisionMouse?
Candidate[377]: We just kept a different mode where the user control the cursor and also move drag the applications. It is the third mode where I and hand works together. Basically, the media pipe face mesh and media pipe hand mesh die works simultaneously parallel to each other.
Interviewer[384]: Understood. How did you evaluate the usability and accuracy of VisionMouse with real users?
Candidate[402]: And, basically, we test it ourselves how easy it is to control the cursor. Based on our feedback, we improved it and also validated it ourselves, and that's how we did it.
Interviewer[409]: Sounds practical. How did you handle error cases or misdetections during use?
Candidate[436]: There are few cases be because each person can look at one point because of the edge. And there are edge cases where the person needs to be controlled at the at the corner of the screen. That was something which was hard. We gave it like, some other tools to calibrate it properly.
Interviewer[444]: I see, that sounds challenging. How did you decide which gestures to prioritize for the hand recognition?
Candidate[458]: It is only prioritized based on the things we did, like, dragging or scrolling or scroll up, scroll down basic features which are included in the project.
Interviewer[464]: Makes sense. What were some of the biggest technical challenges you faced building VisionMouse?
Candidate[479]: Basically, it was really difficult to actually project three gaze system. But we somehow did it with the year aspect ratio and 16 calibration.
Interviewer[486]: Right. How did you go about testing and validating the 3D eye gaze tracking accuracy in VisionMouse?
